ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e7724	The next speaker is Mr Singer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e7745	Madam President. Mr State Secretary! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! In March of this year, the political decision on an economic package for more sustainable living was taken. With this law, we have been implementing this economic package for ten years with the establishment of a housing investment bank and with the most important reform of the housing public utility law. Mr Schrangl, of course, amendments have been requested and made during the review phase. We have also taken into account the corresponding amendment in the Building Committee. First word to the housing investment bank: The aim of this bank is to boost the housing market by transferring loans from supranational European donors of € 700 million to non-profit and commercial builders. A total investment power of €5.7 billion is expected. According to calculations, builders can build about 30 000 new homes for 68 000 people over the next five to seven years with the additional resources. This, of course, helps us to meet the rising demand mainly in the agglomerations. Measures such as this housing package are crucial to the functioning of the housing market, as more apartments basically increase the liquefaction of housing. It's important to me to make a few points. - First of all: The housing investment bank is not a state but a private bank, which is borne by the existing building savings banks and the housing banks. No means of taxation are used, the State is involved in liability. We create very, very slim structures with the settlement of the loans through existing building savings banks and residential banks. It is also very important for me to point out that the return flows from these loans are returning to the system. That is, with these funds, new loans can then be granted again. Ladies and gentlemen! The aim is to create more manageable living space. The aim is also to stimulate the labour market, with the creation and maintenance of 20 000 jobs. Ladies and gentlemen! The second part of this amendment concerns the housing benefit law. The main changes made in the short term: Rehabilitation measures will become easier to implement, as the unanimity of tenants will no longer be necessary for an appropriate voluntary increase in conservation and improvement contributions in the event of rehabilitation, but a three-quarters majority will be sufficient. Non-profit housing companies are obliged to check the possibility of reconstitution in the event of extensive renovations. This, of course, reduces costs for additional construction grounds and infrastructure. The construction can be made cheaper with this measure. In addition, the common beneficiaries will have to bring their capital back more quickly into the new building and renovation of dwellings. This reduces the time frame from three to two years. It is also important that a ten-year speculation period should prevent the immediate re-selling of cheaply acquired properties for profit purposes. There are several other points: Clearances in the differentiation between maintenance and improvement work, income-neutral smoothing of maintenance and improvement contributions, compliance regulations, Fit & Clean regulations, regulatory empowerment for the Ministry of Economic Affairs. Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, I would like to thank all those who have worked in this innovation very much. ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e8042	Mr President. Minister! Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Ensuring living and creating sufficient living space: These are two pillars on which safe living and therefore also an essential part of social security is based. As a social-democratic housing speaker, I am very proud of the law that we can present today, because, with its accompanying measures, it really represents a very big reform in the housing public utility law. At the beginning of the Federal Law on the establishment of a housing investment bank, the WBIB, the idea was to provide an additional impetus for housing acquisition and for the construction industry. That's exactly what happened to the WBIB law. The own housing investment bank, created for this purpose, provides funding from the European Investment Bank. This will provide the money and will only be secured by the federal government. Close partner for this initiative is the non-profit housing industry because it stands for the creation of long-term financially viable housing. And that the Austrian housing market is established in a European comparison by cheap renting and high quality is largely the merit of the non-profit housing industry. Negotiations at the parliamentary level – and at this point I would like to thank all those involved very much for the intensive and very good cooperation – have succeeded in presenting a reform package within the framework of the WGG, which benefits all tenants in the field of non-profit housing. This is a total of 925,000 apartments, almost a quarter of the entire housing market. Next point: Security. – From 2016 onwards, tenants will benefit clearly from predictable rents. The maintenance and improvement contribution, the EVB, which represents the recovery stop for the own residential complex, will in future move in a range of 50 cents up to 2 € and build up slowly. Sudden increases of 126 percent, as they have been possible so far, will be excluded in the future. Unused funds flow back to tenants. It also provides sufficient resources for the renovation of the plant. Costs for general renovation will be divided into 20 years in the future and no longer only for 10 years as before. So here, too, monthly housing costs are reduced. In addition, tenants will only have to take care of bagatell repairs in the future. Everything else regulates the maintenance obligation of the landlord, which is comprehensive. Personally, I would like to say from my time as a rental representative that I know how financially difficult it can often be if a sink is broken or the shared stove is broken. This is not possible for a family at all. These concerns are now taken by this law to the tenants. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] There will be more co-determination when it comes to the setting up of child, age or disability-related measures. By a simple majority, tenants will be able to require home-side improvements from the builder in the future. This also creates conditions for the non-profit housing industry, which, in the change of time, already has new ways in the new building. Gender-sensitive residential facilities, “themen buildings”, well-kept housing and so on are already common practice. Another important point is greater transparency. In the future, new rules will apply to housing transactions by providers of responsibility for their own needs. There is a need for unanimity in the Supervisory Board and a mandatory publication. The Ministry of Economic Affairs is still working on compliance rules, which are to be quickly incorporated into the own rules of the public. To sum up, I think you can say: The efforts in new housing are maximised by EU funds. We are also modernizing the non-profit housing sector. The housing costs are predictable. There's more co-determination. And there is a comprehensive maintenance obligation by the landlords. All of this makes this kind of living the safest in the Austrian housing landscape. Thank you.

The cost of liquidity management would move, as has been said, on the basis of current market conditions, at 35 basis points, at the WBIB at 5 basis points, the operating effort, of course, because it is significantly higher in a large-scale commercial bank, at 75 basis points and in the extremely slender structure of the WBIB at 22 basis points and the state and federal liabilities, for which a liability premium is naturally paid – in the case of which it is a federal liability – at 34 basis points in both models. I have already said this in the Committee, we compare 144 basis points in the Business Bank model to 61 basis points in the WBIB model. This is a clear advantage that should also be passed on. But in principle the long-term... – 144 : 61 also comes in our calculation model. [[Abg. Moser: Maybe you can say that in Euro! The speculation with tax money is not only in residential construction, but is therefore prohibited by law. ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e9456	The next item is the speech by Mr Magistrate. It's a kilnauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e9477	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear Seers! In addition to the already discussed housing package, we discussed several applications in the Building Committee last Friday. One of them claimed to prohibit the flat-rate rates for the construction costs and to calculate the actual costs. However, we reject this request for a number of reasons, for example because it would not be useful for organisational and economic reasons to abolish these lump sums. Especially for small housing units in more rural, structurally weak areas, the calculation of administrative costs for each single apartment would greatly increase the administrative burden for itself. Without package, there would be significant additional costs for the tenants. In contrast, the flatrates force the housing providers not to exceed the required administrative costs, thus ensuring that the administrative costs remain below these flat rate rates and thus forcing the housing managers to also manage sparingly. The ratio of flat rate to actual administrative costs shall be reviewed annually by the revision. Studies also confirm the favourable cost structure created by the flat rate rates and which is also much cheaper than in commercial housing. Nor is the request concerning the interest rate of own resources targeted and therefore rejected by us. Such a limitation of own resources interest rate, as required in the application, may even lead to a loss of substance, and it is certainly not in our interest, from which the Dwelling funds and thus perhaps even preventing new projects.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e13142	The next item is the speech by Mr Keck. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e13163	Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! We also discussed in the last Economic Committee a bill on the transposition of EU directives into Austrian law. In concrete terms, these were the Directives on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the provision of pressure equipment already on the market and, on the other hand, the Directive on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the provision of simple pressure containers on the market. The implementation of these two directives now leads to a new law, the printing equipment law. This recast replaces the already existing boiler law and should essentially lead to further support for the free movement of goods in the EU internal market. What are the essential contents of this law? Firstly, the creation of a new legal basis for the implementation of EU harmonisation legislation and the implementation of the "New Legal Framework" for market surveillance. Secondly, the maintenance of the high level of safety for pressure-leading devices already in operation in Austria. And thirdly, the harmonised administrative provisions, which means that procedures which are in the legal relationship between the notifying authority, the market surveillance authority, the audit bodies and the economic operators concerned are being re-regulated. In concrete terms, the conditions of the Austrian authorities with regard to the economic operators concerned – such as the manufacturers of the equipment or the importers of the printing equipment – or the relationship between the economic operators are being remedied and efficiently regulated. In summary, it is to be said that the proposal is to create an appropriate legal basis for the implementation of the common legal framework for the marketing of products in the printing equipment sector in the EU. It is economically sensible. – That is why this bill also receives our approval.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e14039	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This law fundamentally redefines the BIFIE – stated: the Federal Institute for Education Research, Innovation and Development of Austrian Schools – in terms of content and organisation. In terms of content, it should focus on the scientific tasks, conduct applied education research, carry out evaluations and, for us in the National Council, create the national education plan every three years, and – this too is new – the results of research are to be made compulsory. The most prominent task of the BIFIE, namely the implementation of the central matura, the standardized maturity test, is transferred to the ministry. This makes sense because it is a matter of high-end tasks, at least it is very much perceived by the population. From this school year on, all maturants, including those of the BHS, will be subjected to this standardized maturity test. It is therefore of course particularly important to ensure a smooth transition, and this reform is a matter of concern. The changeover is taking place in a structured transition process, in structured implementation steps, including with regard to the organisation. The dual directorate shall be abolished, instead an educational director/director shall be appointed for five years after the invitation to tender has been made, with special reasons for which a constituency may take place. The funding is also put on new legs by starting from basic funding, which is also cut accordingly, instead of using dedicated funds for defined projects. All of this is also taken into account in the Court's recommendation, and it is very welcome that there is a broad majority in this respect too. In this sense, I thank all those who carry this law. Thank you.

The following changes will be made from 2017 onwards: The dual directorate is abolished, an order procedure is also objectiveized; there is only one location, the one in Salzburg; the Supervisory Board is given stronger rights to scientific leadership, and the Scientific Advisory Board is given stronger rights in ensuring quality. The BIFIE reform goes in the right direction, is modern, stands for science and independence. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e16065	Next speaker: Mr Mag. It's a kilnauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e16087	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Part of the federal law now under discussion concerns the eradication of convictions according to previous anti-homosexual provisions. The aim of this law is to eliminate the visible consequences of past criminal offences for homosexuality, namely by eliminating them and removing them from the criminal register. We often hear the accusation that we are only acting on the basis of highest court decisions – but not here, because in this case we are going beyond the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights. In fact, the EGMR has stated that the mere fact that a conviction was based on a criminal provision that was later repealed is normally irrelevant to the evidentity or deletion of the sentence in the criminal register, since it essentially relates to a historical fact. We go beyond this recognition of the EGMR because, for example, this law also allows for the eradication of convictions on the basis of the total ban on homosexuality in force at the time. However, what is not to be made possible is a general and automatic eradication of all convictions under these provisions. This would not be acceptable, for in the course of which acts of action, which are still punishable today, were also punished, rape, for example. But, of course, every individual case should be examined on request, because it is important that only those convictions that are based on behaviour that are no longer punishable. On the withdrawal of judgments: Criminal laws always represent the ruling basic consensus in a society. Society has now developed further, which is also reflected in the adaptation of the various laws. Many discriminations have already been removed. What we cannot change, however, is the earlier view of the fundamental consensus of a society. That's a historical fact. If you look at the past with the knowledge of the present, you will always come to decisions other than at the time of the decision itself. We can now only eliminate the existing consequences of previous decisions according to today's state of knowledge, and we do so with this law.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e16808	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The current Novelle Court Fees will take a reform in the area of court fees – my predecessor has mentioned this and has also mentioned it positively – which will lead to administrative simplifications, clarifications, but also, above all, to reductions in the said fees, which are particularly interested in the citizens. The Ministry of Justice estimates that more than €5 million of fees are saved per year for the citizen, and that is something. The aim of this amendment is to re-regulate the fees in the appeal proceedings in general outside of disputed proceedings; it is estimated at about €1.3 million in savings. In comparison with the applicable legal situation, a reduction in court fees is expected to occur, particularly in cases of execution and insolvency proceedings, as well as in the case of maintenance and civil service matters. For example, in the case of labour disputes, the exemption limit will be increased from €1 450 to €2 500. This means that in future more labour law procedures are exempt from fees than before. It is estimated that this will save people €300 000 in fees. In the case of the basic book registration fee, clarifications are made, which in particular in the case of subscriptions and subscriptions of properties also bring a savings of about € 1 million. The aim of the amendment is to reduce the fees for company book surveys in line with an EU directive. This is supposed to bring about €2.5 million in savings. They see that, in particular, smaller companies should be able to facilitate access to basic data in the company's book and that the overall burden on the population should be eased. And it's already an important step, at a time when people say: Everything gets more expensive! to reduce fees. That's positive. The law becomes 1st. In January 2016, something is immediately coming into force. But there is also a innovation. In many European languages, so-called diacritical signs are used; in French, Spanish, Hungarian, Croatian, Serbian and other languages. These are small characters such as strokes, hooks or arches in conjunction with a letter, and the representation of these signs has not yet been technically possible. If a Frenchman who had an accent in his name or a Hungarian or a Serb founded a company and wanted to write their names correctly, that was not possible until now. This is technically possible now, and this is done without any additional court fees. I think this is a positive signal because we have many entrepreneurs from Central and Eastern Europe who are active in Austria, and also from other European countries who have such diacritical signs in their names. I think that this will lead to a positive appreciation of these people, their commitment is rewarded by their ability to write their name, their company name correctly, and this also underlines that we want such entrepreneurs, who are active in Austria, in particular in Vienna, there are such companies. This also means that there is an internationalisation of the economy, and I think this is very positive. On the other hand, Austria is also the largest investor in these countries in Central and Southern Europe, and this is a very good signal. It is also positive for the Austrian ethnic groups, who often have such diacritical signs in their names. This is also a signal to support the Culture and the language of the Austrian ethnic groups. Thank you.

Banking supervisory and resolution authorities are increasingly taking on greater dimensions, both at national and European level. We now have the Financial Market Supervision, the Austrian National Bank, a National Banking Authority, a European Single Resolution Committee and, finally, the ECB. By the way, the bank levy must be reformed or even abolished. In any case, there must be a 100% credit for the contributions from the funds to the bank.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e18714	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In connection with the present legal accompanying measures to the so-called Bank Rehabilitation and Resolution Act, but also Deposit Guarantee and Investor Remuneration Act, as Mr Fuchs has already mentioned, is very much about the subject of the Banking Union. A stable and, of course, competitive banking industry is very important both for the functioning of the economy and for the trust of local savers, our taxpayers and taxpayers. New regulations must therefore be designed with care and professionalism. This means that too many regulations make no sense in this sector of the economy, and too high taxes make no sense in this sector. That is why we have great understanding of the issues relating to a distorting double taxation of our bank tax, which Mr Fuchs referred to earlier, but we know the subject in the best hands of our Minister for Finance. And also in this sector... [[Abg. Krainer: What's double about it?]] Privatissimum likes to follow up. – In this sector too, legislation should carefully take one step after another. The issue of the Banking Union – and this has already been mentioned – is to make the financial system in Europe even safer. However, what we do not want at the same time is to turn the risk of foreign banks, ladies and gentlemen, on Austrian savers. Let's take a look at: What has the implementation of the European Banking Union been like so far? – Stage 1 – also mentioned – the so-called single banking supervision has now been decided and implemented in all EU Member States. Stage 2 looks a little different: the uniform rules for bank restructuring and bank settlement – also a very good and welcome project by us. Unfortunately, this level 2 has only been implemented in about half of the EU Member States. The same applies to Stage 3 for the harmonisation of deposit guarantee systems. The aim of this harmonisation is to introduce uniform criteria and, above all, uniform quality standards in the deposit guarantees of each Member State. Unfortunately, this is only implemented in about half of the European countries. We are therefore clearly opposed at this stage to taking the next step. And you know the subject: In summer, in the so-called five-president report, the idea arose of creating a global deposit guarantee at all, i.e. securing all deposits from a security stop. Of course it cannot be, ladies and gentlemen, that Austrian savers – although only indirectly – are liable for foreign assets, although in these countries, as has been done, Stages 2 and 3 of the Banking Union have not yet been implemented, and therefore the risk in the banking landscape is of course far greater than in Austria. Summary: We need to protect the interests of Austrian savers as well as the interests of Austrian taxpayers. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, a clear yes to the implementation of the banking union, but a clear no to the early integration of the savings deposit facility. The investment of these funds will begin where the possibilities of the Alternative Financing Act will end more or less. We are talking here about business phases where growth, expansion, establishment, turnaround investments in the millions and no longer in the area of a few hundred thousand euros, as perhaps covered by crowd investment. Therefore, the previously mentioned private investor restriction in AIFMG should be depleted. Of course, it would be best for us to remove this minimum amount of investment. But a compromise would be a reduction to a scale of €30 000, so as to close this virtual gap between the Alternative Finance Act and the Alternative Investment Fund Manager Act. This threshold of € 100,000 is also well meant, it is intended to serve investor protection, but this may cause the exact opposite. ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00107_d7e19295	Mr President. Mr. Minister! High house! The Government template 897 of the Supplements updates and adapts the Transparency Directive. Like many other EU directives, this Directive also serves to achieve a broad harmonisation within the EU. Among other things, it is about harmonizing the requirements of issuers – these are institutions that issue securities or similar instruments on the money or capital markets for the purpose of acquiring capital – or having them issued through a banking consortium. It is also about improving the transparency of participations in listed companies and expanding existing sanctions against reporting agents when infringements occur. It is essential that it is clear and comprehensible who and above all which capital structure is behind the issuer whose securities are admitted to trading on the market. It is also important that sanctions are dissuasive enough if the Transparency Directive is not complied with. That is, as I mean, quite implemented here. In the event of non-compliance with information to be published, there are high penalties. Technically, there are now criminal offences, which are regulated by European law and those whose sanctions remain in Austrian law. The main objective is to provide information to supervisory authorities and market participants. These penalties imposed by the Financial Market Supervisory Board flow to the Federation, the Financial Market Supervision also cooperates with the other European Supervisory Authorities and publishes the penalties imposed. I think that's also a deterrent. Finally, it's always about a lot of money. In this respect, there cannot be enough transparency and sanctions. This is for the protection of taxpayers and taxpayers. [[Abg. Kogler: Fulminant opening!]] For this increased protection, I would like to vote, Mr Kogler.

Where these cover contributions come and how they are used is also regulated by this law. This measure, which has already been mentioned, implements a recommendation from the Court of Auditors, but also from the Financial Procuratorship. This example shows us quite clearly that the privatisation of state enterprises, as it did between 2000 and 2005, was not without problems and now has great implications for us. That is to say, we have to do with cost rates here, with maximum costs fixed in 2004 and adjusted in a moderate manner eight years later, in 2012. Until 2012, they were not even adapted to inflation. Now we can ask ourselves whether these so-called maximum rates were cost-effective or not. Those who have to do with matter know that they were not, and unfortunately they were not on all levels. In this respect, it is very welcome that this Article 15a agreement should now be adapted and that at least an attempt is being made to make the rates of reality close to the federal side, but also to the federal states. They will still not be cost-effective – to know that, for example, one only needs to talk to accommodation NGOs – but this is a first important step. There was a press conference of young refugees or young adults who came to Austria years ago as refugees. Among other things, there was a 17-year-old Afghan who told him that he was on his way as a minor and fled Afghanistan from the Taliban. He was then declared full-aged in the asylum process, now sitting in the country, in Styria, in an asylum home with loud adult men, and has neither the opportunity to learn German nor to attend a school, nor to take an education. And something like that shouldn't actually happen. That is exactly why I have tabled this request to treat minor unaccompanied protection seekers in such a way as minor protection for children and young people in Austria are also being treated. This request was unfortunately rejected with the votes of the government parties. I cannot understand why young people who need the same help need the same support, who are still without parents in Austria and who still have the difficulty of foreign language for them, should not be given the same care as other children or young people who need support. To this end, I would like once again to urge colleagues from the opposition parties to reconsider their voting behaviour, because it is not fair to justify the fact that children and young people are being given an uneven aid, an uneven support. And to the request of the NEOS: We also support this, because it makes a lot of sense to think that the refugee supply of a profit-oriented company, ORS GmbH, was overreacted. The Minister of the Interior announced that this company made a profit of € 1 million last year, by the way, from taxpayers' money. – So much to earn on refugees. Those of you who were in Traiskirchen and have seen the situation there will know that ORS is supposed to manage this refugee camp there and that the contract also has a 24-hour care for unaccompanied children and youthful refugees – and nothing of this can be seen. In this sense, it makes sense here to rethink the voting behaviour and also to rethink the award to ORS and to overshadow the support of non-profit-oriented companies seeking protection, but above all human rights NGOs who have a lot of experience in this field. Thank you.
